\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{D}^{4}\Psi(x)[h^{\otimes 4}]\right\rvert\leq 3\gamma\rho^{2}s^{2}+4r\beta\rho s^{2}+12r^{2}s^{2}+3s^{4}+6\delta^{2}\rho^{4}+6r^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6(\delta^{2}\rho^{4}+r^{4}+s^{4}+r^{2}s^{2}+\delta\rho^{2}s^{2}+\delta r\rho s^{2})\leq 6{\bigl((\delta\rho)^{4}+r^{4}+s^{4}+r^{2}s^{2}+(\delta\rho)^{2}s^{2}+r^{2}s^{2}+(\delta\rho)^{2}s^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6{\bigl((\delta\rho)^{2}+r^{2}+s^{2}\bigr)}^{2}=6{\bigl(\mathrm{D}^{2}\Psi(x)[h,h]\bigr)}^{2}\,.\qed

7.4.1
Linear constraints: strong self-concordance and symmetry

We relate SSC and symmetry to well-studied terms in the field of optimization,

\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(\sqrt{D_{x}}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}

and
{\|D_{x,h}^{\prime}\|}_{D_{x}^{-1}}^{2}

.

(7.15)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(iv)}}{=}4\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\cdot{\bigl({\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}[D_{x}^{-1}]_{ii}(\mathrm{D}D_{x}[h])_{i}^{2}\bigr)}\,,

x^{\mathsf{T}}(A\circ B)y=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(x)A\textup{{Diag}}(y)B^{\mathsf{T}}\bigr)}

(Lemma
A.2
), (ii) follows from
P_{x}^{(2)}\preceq\Sigma_{x}

(Claim
B.3
)
7
7

7

Even though this lemma is proven for leverage scores, the proof there
can be extended to any orthogonal projection matrices.
, (iii) uses

As for the second claim,

\displaystyle\max_{h:{\|h\|}_{g(x)}=1}{\|A_{x}h\|}_{\infty}=\max_{h}\max_{i\in[m]}\left\lvert\frac{a_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}h}{s_{i}}\right\rvert=\max_{i\in[m]}\max_{u:{\|u\|}_{2}=1}\left\lvert\frac{a_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}g(x)^{-1/2}u}{s_{i}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

Now we establish SSC and compute the symmetry parameters of metrics

Proof of Lemma

Logarithmic barrier

with
{\|A_{x}(y-x)\|}_{\infty}\leq 1

is contained in
\mathcal{D}_{g}^{\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x})}}(x)
, where
\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x})=\textup{{Tr}}(P_{x})\leq d
.
Therefore,
\tilde{g}(x):=40\sqrt{m}g(x)=40\sqrt{m}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x}

is SSC with the symmetry parameter
\bar{\nu}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{m}d)
.

Vaidya metric
: Consider the metric without scaling:
g(x):=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}

with
D_{x}=\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}
. Then, using
Anstreicher 1997

in (i) below

\displaystyle\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:helper4Diagonal}-2}}{=}{\Bigl(\max_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\|A_{x}h\|}_{\infty}}{{\|h\|}_{g(x)}}\Bigr)}^{2}\underset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,,

(7.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}[D_{x}^{-1}]_{ii}\,(\mathrm{D}D_{x}[h])_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(ii)}}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{m}[\Sigma_{x}^{-1}]_{ii}(\mathrm{D}\Sigma_{x}[h])_{i}^{2}\underset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:usefulFactLewis}-3}}{\leq}4h^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x}h\leq 4{\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\,.

Putting these back to Lemma
5.4
-1,

\displaystyle{\|g(x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}g(x)[h]\,g(x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|}_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\,{\bigl({\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}[D_{x}^{-1}]_{ii}(\mathrm{D}D_{x}[h])_{i}^{2}\bigr)}\leq 20\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}{\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\,.

Thus,
\tilde{g}(x):=22\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}g(x)=22\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bigl(\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}\bigr)}A_{x}

is SSC. For the
\bar{\nu}
-symmetry, Lemma
5.4
-2
implies that for
y\in\mathcal{D}_{g}^{1}(x)
,

{\|A_{x}(y-x)\|}_{\infty}^{2}\leq\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\underset{\text{\eqref{eq:28-1}}}{\leq}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,.

Also, Lemma
5.4
-3 implies that
y
with
{\|A_{x}(y-x)\|}_{\infty}\leq 1

is contained in
\mathcal{D}_{g}^{\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x})}}(x)
, where

\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x})=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}\bigr)}=\textup{{Tr}}(\Sigma_{x})+d\leq 2d\,.

Therefore,
\tilde{g}(x)
satisfies
\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{g}}^{1}(x)\subset K\cap(2x-K)\subset\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{g}}^{\sqrt{44(md)^{1/2}}}(x)
,
so
\tilde{g}
is
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{md})
-symmetric.

Lewis-weight metric
: Consider the unscaled version first:

g(x)=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}
. By Lemma
5.4
-1

\displaystyle{\|g(x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}g(x)[h]\,g(x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|}_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(W_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[W_{x}]_{ii}}\,{\bigl({\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}[W_{x}^{-1}]_{ii}(\mathrm{D}W_{x}[h])_{i}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}8m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}{\bigl({\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}+p^{2}\,{\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\bigr)}\leq{\bigl(8m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}(1+p^{2})\bigr)}\,{\|h\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\,,

where in (i) we used Lemma
B.5
-1 and 3.

For the first part of the
\bar{\nu}
-symmetry, Lemma
5.4
-2
implies that

\max_{h:{\|h\|}_{g(x)}=1}{\|A_{x}h\|}_{\infty}=\sqrt{\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(W_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[W_{x}]_{ii}}}\leq\sqrt{2m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}}\,,

and Lemma
5.4
-3 leads to
K\cap(2x-K)\subset\mathcal{D}_{g}^{\sqrt{d}}(x)

due to

\textup{{Tr}}(W_{x})=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}A_{x}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}A_{x})^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\bigr)}=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}A_{x}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}A_{x})^{-1}\bigr)}=d\,.

Therefore,
16p^{2}m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}
is SSC
with
\mathcal{O}{\bigl(dm^{\frac{2}{p+2}}\bigr)}
-symmetry by Lemma
4.9
.
By setting
p=\mathcal{O}(\log m)
, the claim follows.
∎

7.4.2
Linear constraints: strongly lower trace self-concordance of Vaidya

Let
\theta_{1}(x):=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x}
,
\theta_{2}(x):=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}
,
and
\Gamma_{x}:=\textup{{Diag}}{\bigl(A_{x}g(x)^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\bigr)}
. Recall
g=g_{1}+g_{2}

for a PSD matrix function
g_{1}
and the Vaidya metric
g_{2}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{44}
.

Proof.

For
\overline{g}_{2}:=\theta_{1}+\frac{d}{m}\theta_{2}=\frac{1}{44}\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}g_{2}
,
it follows from
g^{-1}\preceq g_{2}^{-1}=\frac{1}{44}\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\overline{g}_{2}^{-1}

that

\displaystyle 44{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}{\|\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}\overline{g}_{2}^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\|}_{\infty}=\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\max_{i\in[m]}\frac{{\bigl[\sigma{\bigl(\sqrt{\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}}A_{x}\bigr)}\bigr]}_{i}}{{\bigl[\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}\bigr]}_{ii}}\underset{\text{\eqref{eq:28-1}}}{\leq}1\,.\qed

Now we show SLTSC of the Vaidya metric:

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

As
\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{2}(x)[h,h]\succeq 0

by Claim
B.1
, we have

\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{2}(x)[h,h]\bigr)}=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(g^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{2}(x)[h,h]g^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}\geq 0\,.

As for
\theta_{1}
, by Lemma
B.4
-6
\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{1}[h,h]\succeq-16A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(S_{x,h}P_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x})A_{x}-6A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}S_{x,h}^{2}P_{x})A_{x}
,
so

\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{1}(x)[h,h]\bigr)}\geq-16\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x})-6\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}P_{x}S_{x,h}^{2}P_{x})\,.

We first note that
\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}P_{x}S_{x,h})=s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{x}\circ I)s_{x,h}=s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}s_{x,h}={\|h\|}_{\theta_{1}}^{2}
.
Using this,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x})

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}^{1/2}S_{x,h}P_{x}\cdot S_{x,h}P_{x}\Gamma_{x}^{1/2})\leq\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}S_{x,h}P_{x}^{2}S_{x,h}\Gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}})\,\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{x}S_{x,h}^{2}P_{x}\Gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(P_{x}S_{x,h}\Gamma_{x}S_{x,h}P_{x})}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}P_{x}\Gamma_{x}P_{x}S_{x,h})}={\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta_{1}}^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}P_{x}S_{x,h}^{2}P_{x})

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}P_{x}\Gamma_{x}P_{x}S_{x,h})\leq{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}P_{x}S_{x,h})\underset{\text{(i)}}{=}{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta_{1}}^{2}\,.

Putting these together and using Lemma
7.6
,

\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta_{1}(x)[h,h]\bigr)}\geq-22{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta_{1}}^{2}\geq-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|h\|}_{\theta_{1}}^{2}\,,

and it follows from
g_{2}=44\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\left(\theta_{1}+\frac{d}{m}\theta_{2}\right)

that
\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}g_{2}(x)[h,h]\bigr)}\geq-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|h\|}_{g_{2}}^{2}
.
∎

7.4.3
Linear constraints: strongly lower trace self-concordance of Lewis-weight

For
\theta(x):=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}
(i.e., the unscaled version
of
g_{2}
), we write
g_{2}=c\cdot\theta
for a constant
c
,
which will be set to
c_{1}(\log m)^{c_{2}}\sqrt{d}
for some constants

c_{1},c_{2}>0
later. Going forward,
P_{x}
indicates the projection
matrix of
W_{x}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}A_{x}
(i.e.,
P_{x}=P(W_{x}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}A_{x})
).

Lemma 7.7
.

{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}\leq 2c^{-1}m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}
.

Proof.

Note that
0\preceq\Gamma_{x}=\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}g^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\preceq c^{-1}\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}\theta^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})
.
By Lemma
B.5
-1,

{\|\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}\theta^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\|}_{\infty}=\max_{i\in[m]}\frac{{\bigl[\sigma{\bigl(W_{x}^{1/2}A_{x}\bigr)}\bigr]}_{i}}{{\bigl[W_{x}\bigr]}_{ii}}\leq 2m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}\,.\qed

Now we show SLTSC of the Lewis-weight metric:

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

From (
B.5
),

\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta[h,h]\succeq-4A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x,h}^{\prime}S_{x,h}A_{x}+A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x,h}^{\prime\prime}A_{x}
.
Thus,

\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta[h,h])\geq\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\Gamma_{x}(W_{x,h}^{\prime\prime}-4W_{x,h}^{\prime}S_{x,h})\bigr)}=-4\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x,h}^{\prime}S_{x,h})+\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x,h}^{\prime\prime})\,.

As for the first term,
\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x,h}^{\prime}S_{x,h})\leq p\,{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}

follows from (
B.7
) with
\Gamma_{x}
replacing
s_{x,h}^{2}
.

As for the second term
\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x,h}^{\prime\prime})
(i.e., (
B.4
)
with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{x}
), each term there is of the form
\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}\textup{{Diag}}(v))

for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, which can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\big|\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\Gamma_{x}\textup{{Diag}}(v)\bigr)}\big|

\displaystyle=|\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}W_{x}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\textup{{Diag}}(v))|\leq\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma_{x}^{2}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}})}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(v)W_{x}^{-1}\textup{{Diag}}(v)\bigr)}}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(W_{x})}{\|v\|}_{W_{x}^{-1}}=\sqrt{d}{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|v\|}_{W_{x}^{-1}}\,.

Then, we obtain
|\textup{{Tr}}(\Gamma_{x}W_{x,h}^{\prime\prime})|\lesssim\sqrt{d}{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}

for
p=\mathcal{O}(\log m)
by using this inequality together with the norm
bounds in Lemma
B.7
.

Putting things together, we conclude that

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta[h,h])

\displaystyle\gtrsim-p{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}-\sqrt{d}{\|\Gamma_{x}\|}_{\infty}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}\gtrsim-c^{-1}\sqrt{d}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}\,,

where the last line follows from Lemma
7.7
.
Therefore, there exists positive constants
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
such
that
\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}\theta[h,h])\geq-c^{-1}d_{1}(\log m)^{d_{2}}\sqrt{d}{\|h\|}_{\theta}^{2}
,
which implies

\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{2}g_{2}[h,h])\geq-c^{-1}d_{1}(\log m)^{d_{2}}\sqrt{d}{\|h\|}_{g_{2}}^{2}\,.

By taking
c=d_{1}(\log m)^{d_{2}}\sqrt{d}
, the metric
g_{2}=c\theta=d_{1}(\log m)^{d_{2}}\sqrt{d}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}

is SLTSC.
∎

7.4.4
Linear constraints: strongly average self-concordance

We proceed with a general form of the metric
g(x)=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}

with a diagonal matrix
0\prec D_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Then we provide computational
lemmas used when proving SASC of barriers for the linear constraints.

We pick any
g^{\prime}:\textup{{int}}(K)\to\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d}
such that
\bar{g}:=g+g^{\prime}\succ 0
. By
affine invariance, we may assume
\bar{g}(x)=I
and
x=0
. Note
that
g(x)\preceq I_{d}
, and
z
equals
rh/\sqrt{d}
for
h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})

in law. Applying Taylor’s expansion to
{\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}
at

z=x
(as in the proof of Lemma
4.15
), for some

p_{z}\in[x,z]

\displaystyle\big|{\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}}{d}\,{\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\underbrace{|\mathrm{D}g(x)[h^{\otimes 3}]|}_{\eqqcolon\textsf{A}}+\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\underbrace{|\mathrm{D}^{2}g(p_{z})[h^{\otimes 4}]|}_{\eqqcolon\textsf{B}}\Bigr)}\,.

It suffices to show that
|\mathrm{D}g(x)[h^{\otimes 3}]|=\mathcal{O}(d^{1/2})

and
|\mathrm{D}^{2}g(p_{z})[h^{\otimes 4}]|=\mathcal{O}(d)
with high probability.

Term
A
.

By (
7.15
), we have
\mathrm{D}g(x)[h^{\otimes 3}]=-2s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}S_{x,h}s_{x,h}+s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x,h}^{\prime}s_{x,h}
.
Let
a_{i}
denote the
i
-th row of
A_{x}
for
i\in[m]
, and
define two polynomials in
h
as follows:

P_{1}(h):=s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}S_{x,h}s_{x,h}=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x}S_{x,h}^{3})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}d_{i}\,(a_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}h)^{3}\,,\quad\text{and}\quad P_{2}(h):=s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x,h}^{\prime}s_{x,h}\,.

(7.17)

By Lemma
C.1
,
D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}^{1/2}\preceq P(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})

and thus

\max_{i\in[m]}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}={\|\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\|}_{\infty}\leq\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\,.

(7.18)

By Lemma
C.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(h)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}{\Bigl[\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{m}d_{i}(a_{i}\cdot h)^{3}\Bigr\}^{2}\Bigr]}=9\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}{\|d_{i}^{1/3}a_{i}\|}^{2}{\|d_{j}^{1/3}a_{j}\|}^{2}\langle d_{i}^{1/3}a_{i},d_{j}^{1/3}a_{j}\rangle+6\sum_{i,j}\langle d_{i}^{1/3}a_{i},d_{j}^{1/3}a_{j}\rangle^{3}

\displaystyle=9\cdot 1^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,D_{x}^{1/2}\underbrace{D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}^{1/2}}_{\preceq P(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})\preceq I_{m}}D_{x}^{1/2}\,\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,1+6\sum_{i,j}d_{i}d_{j}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\|}_{\infty}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,D_{x}\bigr)}+\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i,j}d_{i}d_{j}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\,\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x})+\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j}\textup{{Tr}}(d_{j}a_{j}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}a_{j})

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}2\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\,\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x})\leq 2d\,\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\,,

(7.19)

where (i) follows from
A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}\preceq I_{d}
and
\sum_{j}\textup{{Tr}}(d_{j}a_{j}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}a_{j})\leq\sum_{j}\textup{{Tr}}(d_{j}a_{j}^{\mathsf{T}}a_{j})=\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x})
.

Another polynomial
P_{2}(h)
requires a different strategy for bounding

\mathbb{E}[P_{2}(h)^{2}]
for each barrier. This polynomial vanishes for
the log-barrier, while the Vaidya and Lewis-weight metrics requires
rather involved tasks for bounding
\mathbb{E}[P_{2}(h)^{2}]
.

Term
B
.

Due to (
B.6
) (with
W_{x}
replaced by
D_{x}
),

|\mathrm{D}^{2}g(p_{z})[h^{\otimes 4}]|
consists of three polynomials:

\bar{P}_{3}(h):=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{p_{z}}S_{p_{z},h}^{4})\,,\quad\bar{P}_{4}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{p_{z},h}^{\prime}S_{p_{z},h}^{2})\,,\quad\bar{P}_{5}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{p_{z},h}^{\prime\prime}S_{p_{z},h}^{2})\,.

(7.20)

For each
i=3,4,5
, we define
P_{i}(h)
by
\bar{P}_{i}(h)
with

p_{z}
replaced by
x
. For the log-barrier,
\bar{P}_{3}(h)

only matters since
D_{(\cdot)}=I_{m}
. For the Vaidya metric,
\bar{P}_{4}(h)

and
\bar{P}_{5}(h)
can be bounded by multiples of
\bar{P}_{3}(h)
.
For the Lewis-weight metric, each
\bar{P}_{i}
requires a different
procedure for bounding
\mathbb{E}[\bar{P}_{i}(h)^{2}]
. Moreover, we can
show
\bar{P}_{i}(h)\lesssim P_{i}(h)
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{3}(h)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[m]}\mathbb{E}[d_{i}d_{j}\,(a_{i}\cdot h)^{4}(a_{j}\cdot h)^{4}]\underset{\textup{CS}}{\leq}\sum_{i,j}d_{i}d_{j}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(a_{i}\cdot h)^{8}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(a_{j}\cdot h)^{8}]}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}{\Bigl(\sum_{i}d_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{4}\Bigr)}^{2}\leq\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{4}\,{\Bigl(\sum_{i}d_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\Bigr)}^{2}\underset{\text{(ii)}}{\leq}d^{2}\max_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{4}\,,

(7.21)

where we used
a_{i}\cdot h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,{\|a_{i}\|}^{2})
in (i),
and
\sum_{i}d_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}=\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x})\leq\textup{{Tr}}(I_{d})

in (ii).

We now show SASC of the three barriers for linear constraints, using
this proof outline.

SASC of log-barriers.

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

Set
g(x)=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}

(with
D_{x}=I_{m}
). By (
7.18
),

\max_{i\in[m]}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\leq\max[\sigma(A_{x}^{1/2})]_{i}\leq 1\,.

As for the term
\mathsf{A}
, it suffices to bound
P_{1}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{3})
.
Since
\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d
by (
7.19
), by Lemma
5.8

with
t=(2e)^{3/2}\vee{\bigl(\frac{2e}{3}\log\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\bigr)}^{3/2}

and
r_{1}(\varepsilon):=\varepsilon(2\sqrt{60}t)^{-1}
, we have that for any

r\leq r_{1}(\varepsilon)
,

\text{Event }B_{1}:\quad\mathbb{P}_{h}{\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,|P_{1}(h)|\geq\varepsilon\Bigr)}\leq\varepsilon\,.

As for the term
\mathsf{B}
, recall
\mathbb{P}_{z}{\bigl({\|z\|}\geq-r\cdot 2\log\varepsilon\bigr)}\leq\varepsilon

and call this event
B_{2}
. We take
r_{2}(\varepsilon)
so that
1-2r_{2}\log\varepsilon\leq 1.1
,
which ensures
{\|z\|}\leq 2r
conditioned on
B_{2}^{c}
for
r\leq r_{2}
.
Next, we establish coordinate-wise closeness of
s_{x}
at close-by
points. Let
x_{t}=x+\frac{tr}{\sqrt{d}}h
, and
s_{t}=Ax_{t}-b
.
For
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\left\|S_{0}^{-1}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}s_{t}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|A_{x}h\|}_{\infty}\leq\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|h\|}_{g(x)}\leq\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|h\|}={\|z\|}\,,

and conditioned on
z\in B_{2}^{c}
we know
{\|z\|}\leq 2r\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\leq 0.1

for
r\leq r_{2}
. Hence,

\max_{i\in[m]}\Big|\frac{s_{p,i}-s_{x,i}}{s_{x,i}}\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left\|S_{0}^{-1}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}s_{t}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 0.1\,,

and thus
1.2\geq s_{x,i}/s_{p,i}\geq 0.9
for all
i\in[m]
(i.e.,

S_{p}^{-1}\preceq 1.2S_{x}^{-1}
).

Using this, we bound
\bar{P}_{3}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{4})
by a multiple
of
P_{3}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{4})
as follows:

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{4})

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}(h^{\mathsf{T}}A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{p,h}S_{p}^{-2}S_{p,h}Ah)\leq 2\textup{{Tr}}(h^{\mathsf{T}}A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{p,h}S_{x}^{-2}S_{p,h}Ah)=2\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{2}S_{p,h}^{2})\leq 4\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{4})\,.

Hence,
\mathbb{E}[\bar{P}_{3}(h)^{2}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[P_{3}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d^{2}

by (
7.21
). Using Lemma
5.8

with
t=(2e)^{2}\vee{\bigl(\frac{2e}{4}\log\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\bigr)}^{3/2}

and taking
r_{3}(\varepsilon):=(\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{c_{1}t}})^{1/2}
, we obtain

\text{Event }B_{3}:\quad\mathbb{P}{\Bigl(\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\cdot 16\bar{P}_{3}(h)\geq\varepsilon\Bigr)}\geq\varepsilon\,,

Combining bounds on
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
conditioned on
\cap_{i}B_{i}^{c}
,
we have with probability at least
1-3\varepsilon

\big|{\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\big|\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{r^{2}}{d}\quad\text{for any }r\leq\min_{i}r_{i}(\varepsilon)\,.

By replacing
3\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon
, the claim follows.
∎

SASC of Vaidya metric.

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

Set
g(x)=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}

with
D_{x}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}(\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})
. By
(
7.18
) and (
7.16
),

\max_{i\in[m]}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\leq\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\leq 1\,.

Term
A
.

As
\mathsf{A}
consists of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
(see (
7.17
)),
we show
\mathbb{E}[P_{i}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d
for
i\in[2]
, which by Lemma
5.8

implies
|\mathsf{A}|\leq\sqrt{d}
w.h.p. As for
P_{1}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x}S_{x,h}^{3})
,
we have
\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(h)]^{2}\lesssim d
from (
7.19
).

As for
P_{2}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(D_{x,h}^{\prime}S_{x,h}^{2})
, our approach is similar
to
Chen et al. 2018
. By Lemma
B.4
,

\displaystyle|P_{2}(h)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl(\textup{{Diag}}{\bigl((\Sigma_{x}-P_{x}^{(2)})\,s_{x,h}\bigr)}\,S_{x,h}^{2}\Bigr)}\Big|\leq|P_{1}(h)|+|\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{3})|+\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,\big|\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}^{(2)}s_{x,h})\,S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}\big|\,.

Since we already established a high-probability bound for both
|P_{1}(h)|

and
|\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{3})|
(which is
P_{1}(h)
for the log-barrier),
we focus on the third term in the RHS.

For
\sigma_{x}:=\textsf{{diag}}\left(P_{x}\right)
and
\sigma_{x,i,j}:=(P_{x})_{ij}
,
it follows from
P_{x}^{2}=P_{x}
that
\sigma_{x,i}=\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(\Sigma_{x}S_{x,h}^{3})

\displaystyle=1^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}s_{x,h}^{3}=\sum_{i}(s_{x,h})_{i}^{3}\sigma_{x,i}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{i}^{3}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}^{(2)}s_{x,h})\,S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{i}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{j}\underset{\text{symmetry}}{=}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{j}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{i}\,.

Combining these leads to

\displaystyle 2\,\textup{{Tr}}(\Sigma_{x}S_{x,h}^{3})+6\,\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}^{(2)}s_{x,h})S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\bigl((s_{x,h})_{i}^{3}+3(s_{x,h})_{i}^{2}(s_{x,h})_{j}+3(s_{x,h})_{i}(s_{x,h})_{j}^{2}+(s_{x,h})_{j}^{3}\bigr)}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\bigl((s_{x,h})_{i}+(s_{x,h})_{j}\bigr)}^{3}\,,

so we handle
\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\bigl((s_{x,h})_{i}+(s_{x,h})_{j}\bigr)}^{3}

instead of
\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}^{(2)}s_{x,h})S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}
, as we
already bounded
\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\textup{{Tr}}(\Sigma_{x}S_{x,h}^{3})=P_{1}(h)-\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{3})
.
Due to
(s_{x,h})_{i}+(s_{x,h})_{j}=(a_{i}+a_{j})^{\mathsf{T}}h
, for
c_{ij}:=a_{i}+a_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{\Bigl[\Bigl\{\sum_{i,j\in[m]}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\bigl((s_{x,h})_{i}+(s_{x,h})_{j}\bigr)}^{3}\Bigr\}^{2}\Bigr]}=\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(c_{ij}\cdot h)^{3}(c_{kl}\cdot h)^{3}]

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:variance-1}}}{=}9\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}{\|c_{kl}\|}^{2}(c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl})+6\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}(c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl})^{3}\,.

(7.22)

As for the first term in (
7.22
), we denote

z_{i}:=\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\|c_{ij}\|^{2}
and
Z:=\textup{{Diag}}{\bigl((z_{i})_{i\in[m]}\bigr)}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}{\|c_{kl}\|}^{2}(c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl})={\Bigl\|\sum_{ij}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}c_{ij}\Bigr\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\Bigl\|\sum_{ij}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}a_{i}\Bigr\|}^{2}+2{\Bigl\|\sum_{ij}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}a_{j}\Bigr\|}^{2}=4{\Bigl\|\sum_{ij}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}a_{i}\Bigr\|}^{2}={\Bigl\|\sum_{i}z_{i}a_{i}\Bigr\|}^{2}

\displaystyle=1^{\mathsf{T}}ZA_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}Z\,1\leq 1^{\mathsf{T}}ZD_{x}^{-1/2}P(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})\,D_{x}^{-1/2}Z\,1\leq 1^{\mathsf{T}}ZD_{x}^{-1}Z\,1\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\,\textup{{Tr}}(Z)\,,

(7.23)

where the last inequality follows from
Z\precsim\Sigma_{x}\preceq\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}D_{x}

due to

\displaystyle z_{i}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}(\|a_{i}\|^{2}+\|a_{j}\|^{2})\lesssim\underbrace{\sigma_{x,i}\|a_{i}\|^{2}+\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\|a_{j}\|^{2}}_{\eqqcolon\mathsf{K}_{i}}\leq\sigma_{x,i}\|a_{i}\|^{2}+\sigma_{x,i}\lesssim\sigma_{x,i}\,.

Moreover, using the bound in
\mathsf{K}_{i}
and
\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}{\|a_{j}\|}^{2}=\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}

\textup{{Tr}}(Z)\lesssim\sum_{i}(\sigma_{x,i}\|a_{i}\|^{2}+\sum_{j}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\|a_{j}\|^{2})=2\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x})\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\,\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x})\leq d\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\,.

Putting this into (
7.23
), we obtain
\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}{\|c_{ij}\|}^{2}{\|c_{kl}\|}^{2}(c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl})\lesssim d^{2}/m
.

As for the second term in (
7.22
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}\,(c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl})^{3}\lesssim\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}\,|c_{ij}\cdot c_{kl}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}\,(a_{i}\cdot a_{k}+a_{i}\cdot a_{l}+a_{j}\cdot a_{k}+a_{j}\cdot a_{l})^{2}\lesssim\sum_{i,j,k,l}\sigma_{x,i,j}^{2}\sigma_{x,k,l}^{2}\,(a_{i}\cdot a_{k})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{ik}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{k}\,(a_{i}\cdot a_{k})^{2}=\sum_{k}\textup{{Tr}}(\sigma_{k}a_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x}a_{k})\leq\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\sum_{k}\textup{{Tr}}(\sigma_{k}a_{k}^{\mathsf{T}}a_{k})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\,\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\Sigma_{x}A_{x})\leq\frac{d^{2}}{m}\,.

This establish a high-probability bound of
\mathcal{O}(d^{2}/m)
on (
7.22
),
implying an
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{d})
-high-probability bound on
\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\big|\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(P_{x}^{(2)}s_{x,h})\,S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}\big|
.

Term
B
.

We show that
s_{x}
and
s_{p_{z}}
are close, and the same holds
for
\sigma_{x}
and
\sigma_{p_{z}}
. For
s_{x}
, following the
argument for the log-barrier, we let
x_{t}:=x+th\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}

and
s_{t}:=Ax_{t}-b
. For
0\leq t\leq 1
,

\displaystyle{\Bigl\|S_{0}^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}s_{t}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr\|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|A_{x}h\|}_{\infty}\underset{\eqref{eq:28-1}}{\leq}\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|h\|}_{A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}D_{x}A_{x}}\leq\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|h\|}={\|z\|}\,.

Conditioned on the high-probability bound of
{\|z\|}\leq 2r\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\leq 0.1

for any
r
less than some
r(\varepsilon)
,

\max_{i\in[m]}\Big|\frac{s_{p,i}-s_{x,i}}{s_{x,i}}\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{1}{\Bigl\|S_{0}^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}s_{t}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr\|}_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 0.1\,,

and thus
1.2\geq s_{x,i}/s_{p,i}\geq 0.9
for all
i\in[m]
(i.e.,

S_{p}^{-1}\preceq 1.2S_{x}^{-1}
). For
\sigma_{x}
, as we have

\Sigma_{x}=\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x})^{-1}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})
, we have
the same closeness between
\sigma_{x,i}
and
\sigma_{p,i}
for
each
i\in[m]
.

Using the formulas in Lemma
B.4
,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{D}^{2}g(p)[h^{\otimes 4}]|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,\Bigl(\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl((\Sigma_{p}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})S_{p,h}^{4}\bigr)}+\underbrace{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h})}_{(*)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p})+\underbrace{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h})}_{\leq\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,{\Bigl(\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl((\Sigma_{p}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})S_{p,h}^{4}\Bigr)}+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}\Sigma_{p}S_{p,h}^{2})+\underbrace{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p})}_{\text{Use Lemma \ref{lem:Kronecker}}}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(ii)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl((\Sigma_{p}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})S_{p,h}^{4}\Bigr)}\underset{\text{(iii)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl((\Sigma_{x}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})S_{x,h}^{4}\Bigr)}=P_{3}(h)\,,

where in (i) we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on
(*)
:

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h})\leq\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}^{2}S_{p,h}^{2})}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h})}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{AM-GM}}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}^{2}S_{p,h}^{2})+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h})\bigr)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\,{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}\Sigma_{p}S_{p,h}^{2})+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p})\bigr)}\,,

(ii) follows from
\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p}S_{p,h}^{2}P_{p})=s_{p,h}^{2}\cdot P_{p}^{(2)}s_{p,h}^{2}\preceq s_{p,h}^{2}\cdot\Sigma_{p}s_{p,h}^{2}\preceq s_{p,h}^{2}\cdot(\Sigma_{p}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m})s_{p,h}^{2}
,
and in (iii) we used coordinate-wise closeness of
s_{x}\leftrightarrow s_{p}

and
\sigma_{x}\leftrightarrow\sigma_{p}
. By (
7.21
),

\mathbb{E}[P_{3}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d^{2}
, and an
\mathcal{O}(d)
-high-probability
bound on
|P_{3}(h)|
(so on
\mathsf{B}
) follows from Lemma
5.8
.
∎

SASC of Lewis-weight.

Proof of Lemma
5.12
.

Set
g(x)=\sqrt{d}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}

(with
D_{x}=\sqrt{d}\,W_{x}
). By (
7.18
) and Lemma
B.5
-1,

\max_{i\in[m]}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\leq\max_{i}\frac{[\sigma(D_{x}^{1/2}A_{x})]_{i}}{[D_{x}]_{ii}}\leq\frac{2m^{\frac{2}{p+2}}}{\sqrt{d}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

Term
A
.

As done for the Vaidya metric, a high-probability bound on
\mathsf{A}

requires
\mathbb{E}[P_{i}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d
for
i=1,2
(see (
7.17
)).
Note that
\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(h)^{2}]\lesssim\sqrt{d}
by (
7.19
).

As for
P_{2}(h)=\sqrt{d}\,s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x,h}^{\prime}s_{x,h}
, we show

\mathbb{E}[P_{2}(h)^{2}]\lesssim\sqrt{d}
. Due to
W_{x,h}^{\prime}=-\textup{{Diag}}(W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{x,h})

(Lemma
B.6
),
P_{2}(h)=-\sqrt{d}s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{x,h})s_{x,h}=-\sqrt{d}\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{x,h})S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle P_{2}(h)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(N_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{x,h})W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}S_{x,h}^{2}\bigr)}=\sqrt{d}\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot h)^{2}(b_{i}\cdot h)\,,

where
b_{i}
is the
i
-th row of
B:=N_{x}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{x}

for
i=1,\dots,m
. By Lemma
C.4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{\Bigl[\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot h)^{2}(b_{i}\cdot h)\Bigr\}^{2}\Bigr]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[m]}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}\|a_{i}\|^{2}\|a_{j}\|^{2}(b_{i}\cdot b_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{i,j}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})(a_{i}\cdot b_{i})(a_{j}\cdot b_{j})+4\sum_{i,j}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}\|a_{i}\|^{2}(b_{i}\cdot a_{j})(a_{j}\cdot b_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+2\underbrace{\sum_{i,j}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{2}(b_{i}\cdot b_{j})}_{=:T_{1}}+4\underbrace{\sum_{i,j}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})(a_{i}\cdot b_{j})(a_{j}\cdot b_{i})}_{=:T_{2}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{1^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,W^{\frac{1}{2}}BB^{\mathsf{T}}W^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,1}_{\eqqcolon N_{1}}+4\cdot\underbrace{1^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}B^{\mathsf{T}})\,W^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}B^{\mathsf{T}})\,1}_{\eqqcolon N_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+4\cdot\underbrace{[1^{\mathsf{T}}\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,W^{\frac{1}{2}}B]\cdot[A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}B^{\mathsf{T}})\,1]}_{\leq N_{1}+N_{2}\text{ by Young's inequality}}+2T_{1}+4T_{2}\,.

As for
N_{1}
, since
B^{\mathsf{T}}B=A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{x}^{2}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{x}\leq p^{2}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}

by Lemma
B.8
-1 and thus
B^{\mathsf{T}}B\precsim(d)^{-1/2}I_{d}
,
Lemma
C.1
ensures
BB^{\mathsf{T}}\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}P(B)\preceq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,I_{m}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle N_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\,W\,\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\bigr)}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,\textup{{Tr}}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}WA_{x})\,{\|\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}})\|}_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

As for
N_{2}
, due to
A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}\preceq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}I_{d}

we have
W^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{\frac{1}{2}}\preceq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}I_{m}

by Lemma
C.1
. Thus,

\displaystyle N_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(\{\textup{{Diag}}(A_{x}B^{\mathsf{T}})\}^{2}\bigr)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in[m]}(a_{i}\cdot b_{i})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i}\|a_{i}\|^{2}\|b_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d}\textup{{Tr}}(BB^{\mathsf{T}})\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(P(B)\bigr)}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

As for
T_{1}
, by Young’s inequality (i.e.,
2(a\cdot b)\leq{\|a\|}^{2}+{\|b\|}^{2}
)

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[m]}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{2}\,{\bigl((w_{j}^{1/2}b_{i})\cdot(w_{i}^{1/2}b_{j})\bigr)}\lesssim\sum_{i,j}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{2}\,(w_{j}{\|b_{i}\|}^{2}+w_{i}{\|b_{j}\|}^{2})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i,j}w_{j}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})^{2}{\|b_{i}\|}^{2}=\sum_{i}{\|b_{i}\|}^{2}\cdot\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl(a_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\Bigl(\sum_{j}a_{j}w_{j}a_{j}^{\mathsf{T}}\Bigr)}a_{i}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}{\|b_{i}\|}^{2}\textup{{Tr}}(a_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}WA_{x}a_{i})\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i}{\|b_{i}\|}^{2}{\|a_{i}\|}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{d}\textup{{Tr}}(BB^{\mathsf{T}})\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

As for
T_{2}
, using
(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})\leq{\|a_{i}\|}{\|a_{j}\|}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[m]}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot a_{j})(a_{i}\cdot b_{j})(a_{j}\cdot b_{i})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i,j\in[m]}w_{i}^{1/2}w_{j}^{1/2}(a_{i}\cdot b_{j})(a_{j}\cdot b_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{1/2}b_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\sum_{j}a_{j}w_{j}^{1/2}b_{j}^{\textbackslash T}a_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i}\textup{{Tr}}(a_{i}w_{i}^{1/2}b_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{1/2}B)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl((A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{1/2}B)^{2}\bigr)}\underset{\text{CS}}{\leq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\textup{{Tr}}(B^{\mathsf{T}}W^{1/2}A_{x}A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W^{1/2}B)\leq\frac{1}{d}\textup{{Tr}}(B^{\mathsf{T}}B)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

Putting all the bounds together, we have
\mathbb{E}[P_{2}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}=\sqrt{d}
.

Term
B
.

We show that for any given
\alpha=\Theta(1)
, each coordinate of

w_{x}/s_{x}^{\alpha}
and
w_{p_{z}}/s_{p_{z}}^{\alpha}
is close.
For
0\leq t\leq 1
, we define
x_{t}:=x+\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}th
, and

s_{t},

w_{t}
in the same fashion. Then for
p=\mathcal{O}(\log m)
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[m]}\Big|\log\frac{(w_{p_{z},i})^{\alpha}}{s_{p_{z},i}}-\log\frac{(w_{x,i})^{\alpha}}{s_{x,i}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\frac{[w_{t,i}]^{\alpha}}{s_{t,i}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\,{\|h\|}_{A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}{\|z\|}\,.

Just as in showing SASC of the Vaidya metric, we can make this bound
arbitrarily small (say
\delta\approx 0
) by conditioning on the high-probability
region where
{\|z\|}\leq r\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\leq 0.01
. Hence,

e^{-\delta}\frac{(w_{x,i})^{\alpha}}{s_{x,i}}\leq\frac{(w_{p_{z},i})^{\alpha}}{s_{p_{z},i}}\leq e^{\delta}\frac{(w_{x,i})^{\alpha}}{s_{x,i}}\,.

(7.24)

We remark that this
\Theta(1)
-multiplicative closeness is still
valid without the
\sqrt{d}
-scaling of
A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x}
.

Using the formula for
\mathrm{D}^{2}(A_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}A_{x})[h^{\otimes 4}]

in (
B.6
),

\displaystyle|\mathrm{D}^{2}g(p)[h^{\otimes 4}]|\lesssim{\bigl(\bar{P}_{3}(h)+|\bar{P}_{4}(h)|+|\bar{P}_{5}(h)|\bigr)}=\bar{P}_{3}(h)+\sqrt{d}\,{\bigl(|\textup{{Tr}}(W_{p,h}^{\prime}S_{p,h}^{3})|+|\textup{{Tr}}(W_{p,h}^{\prime\prime}S_{p,h}^{2})|\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\bar{P}_{3}(h)+\sqrt{d}\underbrace{\big|\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(S_{p,h}^{3}\textup{{Diag}}(W_{p}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{p}W_{p}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{p,h})\bigr)}\big|}_{\eqqcolon T_{1}}+\sqrt{d}\underbrace{|\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}W_{p,h}^{\prime\prime})|}_{\eqqcolon T_{2}}\,,

where in the last line we used the formula for
W_{p,h}^{\prime}
(Lemma
B.6
).

Now we show
\mathbb{E}[\bar{P}_{3}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d^{2}
and
T_{i}\lesssim\sqrt{d}

w.h.p. for
i=4,5
. As for
\bar{P}_{3}
, we have
\bar{P}_{3}(h)\lesssim P_{3}(h)

from the closeness (
7.24
) of
w_{i}/s_{i}^{4}
for
each
i\in[m]
, so
\mathbb{E}[P_{3}(h)^{2}]\lesssim d^{2}\cdot d^{-1}=d

from (
7.21
).

As for
T_{1}
, using the Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\big|\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl(S_{p,h}^{3}W_{p}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\textup{{Diag}}(N_{p}W_{p}^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{p,h})\bigr)}\big|\leq\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{3}W_{p}S_{p,h}^{3})}\sqrt{s_{p,h}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{p}^{1/2}N_{p}^{2}W_{p}^{1/2}s_{p,h}}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{s_{p,h}^{3}W_{p}s_{p,h}^{3}}\sqrt{s_{p,h}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{p}s_{p,h}}\underset{\text{(ii)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{s_{x,h}^{3}W_{x}s_{x,h}^{3}}\sqrt{s_{x,h}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{x}s_{x,h}}=\sqrt{s_{x,h}^{3}W_{x}s_{x,h}^{3}}\cdot d^{-1/4}{\|h\|}_{g(x)}\,,

where in (i) we used
N_{x}\preceq p^{2}I
(Lemma
B.8
),
and in (ii) the closeness of
w_{i}/s_{i}^{6}
and
w_{i}/s_{i}^{2}

established in (
7.24
). As for the first term in the
RHS,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(s_{x,h}^{3}W_{x}s_{x,h}^{3})^{2}]

\displaystyle\underset{\text{CS}}{\lesssim}\sum_{i,j\in[m]}w_{i}w_{j}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(a_{i}\cdot h)^{12}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(a_{j}\cdot h)^{12}]}={\Bigl(\sum_{i}w_{i}\,{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[(a_{i}\cdot h)^{12}]\bigr)}^{2}\Bigr)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Bigl(\sum_{i}w_{i}{\|a_{i}\|}^{6}\Bigr)}^{2}\leq{\Bigl(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\sum_{i}w_{i}\Bigr)}^{2}=\frac{1}{d}\,.

As for the second term, the concentration of the standard Gaussian
guarantees
{\|h\|}_{g(x)}\leq{\|h\|}\lesssim\sqrt{d}
w.h.p. Therefore,

T_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{d}
w.h.p.

As for
T_{2}
, (
B.4
) with
\Gamma_{p}=S_{p,h}^{2}

equals
T_{2}
. Following (
B.8
) with I, II, III,
IV defined in (
B.5
),

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{v=\text{I,II,III,IV}}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(W_{p}S_{p,h}^{4})}{\|v\|}_{W_{p}^{-1}}\underset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(W_{p}S_{p,h}^{4})}\,{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}W_{p})+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{4}W_{p})\bigr)}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(ii)}}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(W_{x}S_{x,h}^{4})}\,{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{2}W_{x})+\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{4}W_{x})\bigr)}\,,

where (i) follows from Lemma
B.7
(i.e.,

{\|v\|}_{W_{p}^{-1}}\lesssim{\|h\|}_{A_{p}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{p}A_{p}}^{2}=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{2}W_{p})

for
v=
I, II, III, and
{\|\text{IV}\|}_{W_{p}^{-1}}\lesssim\textup{{Tr}}(S_{p,h}^{4}W_{p})
),
and (ii) follows from the conditioned event where the closeness of

w_{i}/s_{i}^{2}
at
x
and
z
holds. Since we already established
the high-probability bounds of
d^{-1/2}P_{3}(h)=\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{4}W_{x})\lesssim 1

and
\textup{{Tr}}(S_{x,h}^{2}W_{x})\lesssim\sqrt{d}
, combining these yield

T_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d}
w.h.p.
∎

7.4.5
Quadratic constraints

We show that a
\nu
-SC barrier
\psi(\cdot)=-\log f(\cdot)
satisfies

|\mathrm{D}^{4}\psi(x)[h^{\otimes 4}]|\lesssim\nu^{2}{\|h\|}_{\nabla^{2}\psi(x)}^{2}+\Big|\frac{\mathrm{D}^{4}f(x)[h^{\otimes 4}]}{f(x)}\Big|\,.

Proof of Lemma
5.13
.

Fix
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
x\in\textup{{int}}(K)
,
define
\phi(t):=\psi(x+th)
. Then,

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{f^{\prime}}{f}\,,

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{f^{\prime}}{f}\right)^{2}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime}}{f}=(\phi^{\prime})^{2}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime}}{f}\,,

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=2\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime\prime}f-f^{\prime\prime}f^{\prime}}{f^{2}}=2\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime\prime}}{f}+\frac{f^{\prime\prime}f^{\prime}}{f^{2}}=2\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\phi^{\prime}(\phi^{\prime\prime}-(\phi^{\prime})^{2})-\frac{f^{\prime\prime\prime}}{f}

\displaystyle=3\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}-(\phi^{\prime})^{3}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime\prime}}{f}\,,

\displaystyle\phi^{(4)}

\displaystyle=3(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{2}+3\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}-3(\phi^{\prime})^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}-\frac{f^{(4)}f-f^{\prime\prime\prime}f^{\prime}}{f^{2}}

\displaystyle=3(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{2}+3\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}-3(\phi^{\prime})^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\phi^{\prime}\left(\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}-3\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}+(\phi^{\prime})^{3}\right)-\frac{f^{(4)}}{f}

\displaystyle=3(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{2}+4\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}-6(\phi^{\prime})^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+(\phi^{\prime})^{4}-\frac{f^{(4)}}{f}\,.

Since
|\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}|\leq 2(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{3/2}
(SC of
\phi
) and
\phi^{\prime\prime}\geq\frac{1}{\nu}(\phi^{\prime})^{2}

(the definition of the barrier parameter), which is equivalent to

|\phi^{\prime}|\leq\sqrt{\nu}(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}
, we can directly compute as
follows:

\displaystyle|\phi^{(4)}|

\displaystyle\leq 4\,|\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}|+3\,|(\phi^{\prime\prime})^{2}|+6|\,(\phi^{\prime})^{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}|+|(\phi^{\prime})^{4}|+\Big|\frac{f^{(4)}}{f}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\nu}\,|\phi^{\prime\prime}|^{2}+3\,|\phi^{\prime\prime}|^{2}+6\nu\,|\phi^{\prime\prime}|^{2}+\nu^{2}\,|\phi^{\prime\prime}|^{2}+\Big|\frac{f^{(4)}}{f}\Big|\lesssim\nu^{2}|\phi^{\prime\prime}|^{2}+\Big|\frac{f^{(4)}}{f}\Big|\,.\qed

Using this tool, we study Dikin-amenability of barriers for quadratic
constraints.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

Let us check the last
claim first. By Lemma
4.18
, we may assume that

\phi(x,y)=-\log(l+q^{\mathsf{T}}y-\frac{1}{2}{\|x\|}^{2})\,,

and let
f(x,y)=l+q^{\mathsf{T}}y-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|x\|}^{2}
. For
z=(x,y)\in\textup{{int}}(K)

and
u=(u_{x},u_{y})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}\phi(z)[u]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{f}\,(q\cdot u_{y}-x\cdot u_{x})=\frac{x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y}}{f}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}\phi(z)[u,u]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}+\frac{1}{f}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}\,.

(7.25)

As for the first term in the RHS of (
7.25
),
it holds that for
v=(v_{x},v_{y})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}{\Bigl(\frac{(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}}{f^{2}}\Bigr)}[v]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})}{f^{2}}+2\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}\cdot\frac{x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y}}{f^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}{\Bigl(\frac{(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}}{f^{2}}\Bigr)}[v,v]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\,(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})^{2}}{f^{2}}+4\frac{(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y})}{f^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y})+2\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}}{f^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{6\,(x\cdot u_{x}-q\cdot u_{y})^{2}(x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y})^{2}}{f^{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\,(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})^{2}}{f^{2}}+\frac{4\,(x_{q}\cdot u)(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x_{q}\cdot v)}{f^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\,(x_{q}\cdot u)(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x_{q}\cdot v)+2(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}}{f^{3}}+\frac{6\,(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}}{f^{4}}\,,

where
x_{q}:=(x,-q)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

As for the second term, direct computations lead to

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}{\Bigl(\frac{{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}}{f}\Bigr)}[v]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}(x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}{\Bigl(\frac{{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}}{f}\Bigr)}[v,v]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{f^{3}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}(x\cdot v_{x}-q\cdot v_{y})^{2}+\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{f^{3}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}+\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}\,.

Putting these together, for
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{4}\phi[u,u,v,v]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{2}{f^{2}}\,(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})^{2}}_{\geq 0}+\frac{4}{f^{3}}\,{\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}+2\,(x_{q}\cdot u)(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x_{q}\cdot v)+\frac{(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}}{2}\,{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{6}{f^{4}}\,(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4}{f^{3}}\,\bigg(\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}}_{\text{Use AM-GM}}+2(x_{q}\cdot u)(v_{x}\cdot u_{x})(x_{q}\cdot v)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{f^{2}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}^{2}{\|v_{x}\|}^{2}+\frac{6}{f^{4}}\,(x_{q}\cdot u)^{2}(x_{q}\cdot v)^{2}}_{\text{Use AM-GM}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4}{f^{3}}\,{\bigl({\|u_{x}\|}\,{\|v_{x}\|}\,|x_{q}\cdot v|\,|x_{q}\cdot u|-2|x_{q}\cdot u|\,|x_{q}\cdot v|\,{\|u_{x}\|}\,{\|v_{x}\|}\bigr)}+\frac{2\sqrt{6}}{f^{3}}\,|x_{q}\cdot u|\,|x_{q}\cdot v|\,{\|u_{x}\|}\,{\|v_{x}\|}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{f^{3}}\,{\|u_{x}\|}\,{\|v_{x}\|}\,|x_{q}\cdot v|\,|x_{q}\cdot u|\,{\Bigl(\frac{\sqrt{6}}{2}-1\Bigr)}\geq 0\,.\qed

7.4.6
PSD: convexity and strongly self-concordance

We start with convexity of
\log\det(\nabla^{2}\phi)
for
\phi(X)=-\log\det X
.

Proof of Proposition
5.22
.

Using Lemma
5.20

and
\det{\bigl(M^{\mathsf{T}}(A\otimes A)M\bigr)}=2^{d(d-1)/2}\,(\det A)^{d+1}

(Lemma
A.1
) in the first and second equality below,

\displaystyle\log\det{\bigl(\nabla^{2}\phi(X)\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\log\det{\bigl(M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1})M\bigr)}=\frac{d(d-1)}{2}\,\log 2-(d+1)\,\log\det X\,.

Since
-\log\det X
is convex in
X
(
A.4
),
the convexity of
\log\det{\bigl(\nabla^{2}\phi(X)\bigr)}
also follows.
∎

Observe from the proof that
\log\det{\bigl(\nabla^{2}\phi(X)\bigr)}=\text{const.}+(d+1)\,\phi(X)
.
Differentiating both sides in direction
H
, by (
A.1
)

\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl([\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-1}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\bigr)}=(d+1)\,\mathrm{D}\phi(X)[H]
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl([\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}=-(d+1)\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}H)\,.

(7.26)

We are ready to show SSC of
\phi
.

Proof of Lemma
5.23
.

For
H\in\mathbb{S}^{d}

and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, denote
X_{t}:=X+tH
and
g_{t}:=M^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-1}M
.
Note that

{\bigl\|[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr\|}_{F}^{2}=\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\partial_{t}g_{t}|_{t=0}\,g^{-1}\partial_{t}g_{t}|_{t=0})\,,

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{t}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{=}\partial_{t}{\bigl(M^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-1}M\bigr)}\Big|_{t=0}\underset{\text{(ii)}}{=}-M^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-1}\,\partial_{t}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})|_{t=0}\,(X\otimes X)^{-1}M

\displaystyle=-M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1})(H\otimes X+X\otimes H)(X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1})M

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(iii)}}{=}-M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})M\,,

(7.27)

where (i) follows from Lemma
5.20
, (ii) is due
to (
A.2
), and (iii) follows from
(A\otimes B)(C\otimes D)=(AC)\otimes(BD)

(Lemma
A.1
-3).

Recall that positive semidefinite matrices have unique positive semidefinite
square roots, so
(X\otimes X)^{\frac{1}{2}}=X^{\frac{1}{2}}\otimes X^{\frac{1}{2}}

(due to
(X^{1/2}\otimes X^{1/2})\cdot(X^{1/2}\otimes X^{1/2})=X\otimes X
).
Since
g_{t}=M^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-1/2}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-1/2}M
,
the corresponding orthogonal projection is

P_{t}:=P{\bigl((X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-\frac{1}{2}}M\bigr)}=(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-\frac{1}{2}}Mg_{t}^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{t}\otimes X_{t})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,.

By substituting
\partial_{t}g_{t}\big|_{t=0}
with (
7.27
),

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(g^{-1}\partial_{t}g_{t}|_{t=0}\,g^{-1}\partial_{t}g_{t}|_{t=0})

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}\bigl(g^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})M

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot g^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M}g^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})M

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot g^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl({\bigl[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}Mg^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})\bigr]}^{2}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl({\bigl[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(X\otimes X)^{\frac{1}{2}}P(X\otimes X)^{\frac{1}{2}}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})\bigr]}^{2}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}{\Bigl({\bigl[P\underbrace{(X\otimes X)^{\frac{1}{2}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}+X^{-1}\otimes X^{-1}HX^{-1})(X\otimes X)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{\eqqcolon S}\bigr]}^{2}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}(PSPS)\,.

Using Lemma
A.1
-3,

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\underbrace{X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\otimes I_{d}}_{\eqqcolon A}+\underbrace{I_{d}\otimes X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}}_{\eqqcolon B}\,.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality along with
P^{\mathsf{T}}P=P^{2}=P
and

P\preceq I_{d}
,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}(PSPS)

\displaystyle\leq\textup{{Tr}}((PS)^{\mathsf{T}}PS)\leq\textup{{Tr}}(S^{\mathsf{T}}S)={\|S\|}_{F}^{2}\leq({\|A\|}_{F}+{\|B\|}_{F})^{2}\,.

Using Lemma
A.1
-3,

\displaystyle{\|A\|}_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl((X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\otimes I_{d})\cdot(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\otimes I_{d})\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\otimes I_{d})=\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})\,\textup{{Tr}}(I_{d})=d\,{\|H\|}_{X}^{2}\,,

and similarly
{\|B\|}_{F}^{2}=d\,{\|H\|}_{X}^{2}
. Therefore,
\psi_{X}\leq 2\sqrt{d}

follows from

{\bigl\|[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr\|}_{F}\leq\sqrt{\textup{{Tr}}(PSPS)}\leq 2\sqrt{d}\,{\|H\|}_{X}\,.

To see the optimality of
\mathcal{O}(d^{1/2})
, we recall (
7.26
):

\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl([\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}=-(d+1)\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}H)\,.

Taking supremum on both sides,

\displaystyle\sup_{H:{\|H\|}_{X}=1}\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl([\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\in\mathbb{S}^{d}:\\
{\|X^{-1/2}HX^{-1/2}\|}_{F}=1\end{subarray}}-(d+1)\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=\sup_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{d}:{\|S\|}_{F}=1}(d+1)\,\textup{{Tr}}(S)\,,

and this objective achieves the maximum at
H=-d^{-1/2}X
, with the
supremum being
(d+1)\sqrt{d}
. On the other hand, due to
\textup{{Tr}}(A)\leq d^{1/2}\,{\|A\|}_{F}

for
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,

\displaystyle\textup{{Tr}}{\bigl([\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{d(d+1)}{2}}\cdot{\bigl\|[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{D}^{3}\phi(X)[H]\,[\nabla^{2}\phi(X)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigr\|}_{F}\leq\sqrt{\frac{d(d+1)}{2}}\cdot\psi_{X}{\|H\|}_{X}\,,

and thus by taking supremum on both sides over a symmetric matrix

H
with
{\|H\|}_{X}=1
, it follows that
(d+1)\sqrt{d}\leq\sqrt{\frac{d(d+1)}{2}}\,\psi_{X}

and

\sqrt{2(d+1)}\leq\psi_{X}\,.\qed

7.4.7
PSD: strongly lower trace self-concordance

Direct computation leads to
\mathrm{D}^{2}g(X)[H,H]\succeq 0
(so SLTSC).

Proof of Lemma
5.25
.

For
g(X)=-\nabla^{2}\log\det X
,
recall that
g(X)[H,H]=\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}H)
. Thus for any
V\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}g(X)[H,H,V]

\displaystyle=-\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}VX^{-1}\cdot HX^{-1}H)-\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}H\cdot X^{-1}VX^{-1}\cdot H)

\displaystyle=-2\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-1}H)\,,

and differentiating again,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}g(X)[H,H,V,V]

\displaystyle=4\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}VX^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-1}H)+2\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-1}VX^{-1}H)

\displaystyle=4\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}VX^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})+2\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot X^{-\frac{1}{2}}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}4\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}VX^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})-2\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}VX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot X^{-\frac{1}{2}}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=2\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-1}VX^{-1}VX^{-1}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})\geq 0\,,

(7.28)

where in (i) we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Therefore,
\mathrm{D}^{2}g(X)[H,H]\succeq 0
.
∎

7.4.8
PSD: average self-concordance

We establish a connection to the Gaussian orthogonal ensemble (GOE):
for
d_{s}=d(d+1)/2
and
\textup{{svec}}(H)\sim\mathcal{N}{\bigl(0,\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}}\,g(X)^{-1}\bigr)}
,
we have
\frac{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}{r}X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is the GOE.

Proof of Lemma
5.26
.

Let
h_{X}:=\textup{{svec}}(X^{-1/2}HX^{-1/2})

and
h:=\textup{{svec}}(H)
. It holds that

h_{X}=L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}Mh

due to
h_{X}=\textup{{svec}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})=L\,\textup{{vec}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})=L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\textup{{vec}}(H)=L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}Mh
.
As
h\sim\mathcal{N}{\bigl(0,\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}}\,g(X)^{-1}\bigr)}
,
h_{X}

is a Gaussian with zero mean and covariance

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}}L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}Mg(X)^{-1}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(i)}}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}d}L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}MLN(X\otimes X)N^{\mathsf{T}}L^{\mathsf{T}}M^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\underset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}d}L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}N(X\otimes X)N^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\underset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}d}L(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(X\otimes X)N(X\otimes X)^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{\mathsf{T}}\underset{\text{(*)}}{=}\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}d}LNL^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{(ii)}}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}d}\,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{d}\\
&\frac{1}{2}I_{d(d-1)/2}\end{array}\right]\,,

where (i) follows from Proposition
5.20
,
(*)

follows from Lemma
5.19
, and (ii) follows from

Magnus and Neudecker 1980
that
LNL^{\mathsf{T}}
is a
d_{s}\times d_{s}

diagonal matrix with
d
times
1
and
\frac{1}{2}d(d-1)
times
1/2
.
Precisely, the entries of
h_{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}
corresponding to the
diagonals of
X^{-1/2}HX^{-1/2}
are
1
, and its entries corresponding
to off-diagonals is
1/2
. This is exactly the covariance matrix
of a
d_{s}
-dimensional GOE, so
X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sim\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}G

for the GOE
G
.
∎

Now we show ASC of
d\phi
.

Proof of Lemma
5.27
.

Expand
{\|Z-X\|}_{Z}^{2}:={\|Z-X\|}_{g(Z)}^{2}

at
X
for
Z=X+H
:

{\|Z-X\|}_{Z}^{2}-{\|Z-X\|}_{X}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\,\mathrm{D}^{k}g(X)[H^{\otimes k+2}]\,.

It follows from induction that for
H_{X}:=X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}g(X)[H^{\otimes 3}]

\displaystyle=-2d\,\textup{{Tr}}(X^{-1}HX^{-1}HX^{-1}H)=-2\textup{{Tr}}(H_{X}^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}g(X)[H^{\otimes 4}]

\displaystyle=3!\,d\,\textup{{Tr}}(H_{X}^{4})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{k}g(X)[H^{\otimes(k+2)}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(k+1)!\,d\,\textup{{Tr}}(H_{X}^{k+2})\,.

Putting these back into the series expansion, for
H
the GOE (see
Lemma
5.26
)

\displaystyle{\|Z-X\|}_{Z}^{2}-{\|Z-X\|}_{X}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(k+1)d\,\textup{{Tr}}(H_{X}^{k+2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(k+1)d\cdot{\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\Bigr)}^{k+2}\textup{{Tr}}(H^{k+2})=\frac{r^{2}}{d_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(k+1)\,{\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\Bigr)}^{k}\textup{{Tr}}(H^{k+2})\,.

As for ASC, it suffices to show that
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(k+1){\bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\bigr)}^{k}\,\textup{{Tr}}(H^{k+2})

can be made arbitrarily small. We first control
\sum_{k\geq 2}
:

\Big|\sum_{k\geq 2}(-1)^{k}(k+1){\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\Bigr)}^{k}\textup{{Tr}}(H^{k+2})\Big|\leq\sum_{k\geq 2}(k+1){\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\Bigr)}^{k}d\cdot{\|H\|}_{\text{op}}^{k+2}\,.

By
Vershynin 2018
,
{\|H\|}_{\text{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{d}

holds with high probability, and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 2}(k+1){\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{d_{s}d}}\Bigr)}^{k}d\cdot{\|H\|}_{\text{op}}^{k+2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 2}(k+1)r^{k}\frac{1}{d^{3k/2}}d\cdot d^{\frac{k+2}{2}}\leq\sum_{k\geq 2}(k+1)r^{k}d^{2-k}\,.

By taking
r=\Omega(1)
small enough, we can make this series arbitrarily
small.

Now we bound
\frac{r}{d^{3/2}}\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3})
(
k=1
case). This is
a Gaussian polynomial in
\textup{{svec}}(H)
, so it suffices to show
\mathbb{E}[(\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3}))^{2}]=\mathcal{O}(d^{3})
;
we then use Lemma
5.8
to obtain a high-probability
bound on the Gaussian polynomial
\frac{r}{d^{3/2}}\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3})
. For

H=(H_{ab})\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
,

{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3})\bigr)}^{2}=\sum_{ipq}H_{ip}H_{pq}H_{qi}\cdot\sum_{jrs}H_{jr}H_{rs}H_{sj}=\sum_{ipqjrs}H_{ip}H_{pq}H_{qi}H_{jr}H_{rs}H_{sj}\,,

where each
H_{**}
in the summand is an independent Gaussian with
zero mean and variance
1
or
1/2
(as
H
is the GOE). We can
classify the indices
\{i,p,q,j,r,s\}
into the following types:

\displaystyle 6\text{ distinct indices }

\displaystyle\{a,b,c,d,e,f\}\,,

\displaystyle 5\text{ distinct indices }

\displaystyle\{a,b,c,d,(e,e)\}\,,

\displaystyle 4\text{ distinct indices }

\displaystyle\{a,b,c,(d,d,d)\},\{a,b,(c,c),(d,d)\}\,,

Others

\displaystyle\dots\,,

where for example
\{a,b,c,d,e,f\}
means all indices are different,
and
\{a,b,c,d,(e,e)\}
means that there appear 5 different indices

\{a,b,c,d,e\}
but exists one pair
(e,e)
of the same index. Note
that
\mathbb{E}H_{ip}H_{pq}H_{qi}H_{jr}H_{rs}H_{sj}=\mathcal{O}(1)
is at most
the sixth moment of a standard Gaussian. It implies that toward our
goal of showing
\mathcal{O}(d^{3})
-bound on
{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3})\bigr)}^{2}
,
it suffices to look into only three types of indices above. This is
because the terms from other types contribute at most
\mathcal{O}(d^{3})

to
{\bigl(\textup{{Tr}}(H^{3})\bigr)}^{2}
.

i
p
q
j
s
r

Figure 7.1
:
A structure of indices of
H_{ip}H_{pq}H_{qi}\cdot H_{jr}H_{rs}H_{sj}

For any term with 6 distinct indices, we can always find an ‘uncoupled’

H_{**}
(for example
H_{ab}
) in the summand that is independent
of all the others, so its expectation of the summand is
0
.

For the terms with
5
-distinct indices
\{a,b,c,d,(e,e)\}
, due
to symmetry (see Figure
7.1
) we can further classify
the index
(i,p,q,j,r,s)
into either
(a,b,c,d,e,e)
or
(a,b,e,c,d,e)
.
In both cases ,
H_{ab}
has no coupled Gaussian, so the expectations
of the summand are also
0
.

For
4
-distinct indices, let us first consider
\{a,b,c,(d,d,d)\}
-type
indices. In this case
(i,p,q,j,r,s)
is of the form either
(a,a,a,b,c,d)

or
(a,a,b,a,c,d)
due to symmetry. In both cases,
H_{cd}
has
no coupled Gaussian. Now consider
\{a,b,(c,c),(d,d)\}
-type indices.
Then
(i,p,q,j,r,s)
is of the form either
(a,b,c,c,d,d)
or
(a,c,c,b,d,d)

or
(a,c,d,b,c,d)
. For each case,
H_{ab},H_{cc},H_{ac}
are uncoupled
ones. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}[H_{ip}H_{pq}H_{qi}H_{jr}H_{rs}H_{sj}]=0
whenever
there are at least
4
distinct indices.
∎

Remark 7.8
.

It seems challenging to show
that
\phi
is SASC using the same technique. When
g
is

g=d\,\nabla^{2}(-\log\det X)+g^{\prime}

for other PSD matrix function
g^{\prime}
, we know that
\textup{{svec}}(H_{X})=\textup{{svec}}(X^{-\frac{1}{2}}HX^{-\frac{1}{2}})

follows a Gaussian distribution with zero mean and covariance matrix

M
satisfying

M\preceq\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{d}\\
&\frac{1}{2}I_{d(d-1)/2}\end{array}\right]\,.

A main difference in the SASC setting is that the entries of
h=\textup{{svec}}(H_{X})

might exhibit dependencies, making the previous approach infeasible.
This arises because many fundamental results in the random matrix
theory often presume independence of the entries of a random matrix.
Moreover, our combinatorial argument for the
k=1
case is not feasible
in the presence of such dependencies.

7.5
Examples (
\lx@sectionsign
6
)

7.5.1
Algorithms for PSD sampling

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

We define
g_{X}=g=2(d^{2}g_{1}+g_{2})
,
where

g_{1}(X)=M^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-1}M\qquad\text{and}\qquad g_{2}(X)=22\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\,M^{\mathsf{T}}A_{X}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bigl(\Sigma_{X}+\frac{d}{m}I_{m}\bigr)}A_{X}M\,.

Since
d^{2}g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are SSC,
g
is also SSC due to
Lemma
4.5
and
\mathcal{O}(d^{3}+\sqrt{md^{2}})
-symmetric
8
8

8

Since the dimension is
d_{s}
in the PSD setting, we should replace

d
by
d_{s}=\mathcal{O}(d^{2})
when applying Lemma
5.5
.
due to Lemma
4.11
. As
d^{2}g_{1}
and

g_{2}
is SLTSC and SASC,
g
is LTSC and ASC. Putting these together,
it follows that
g
is
{\bigl(\mathcal{O}(d^{3}+\sqrt{md^{2}}),\mathcal{O}(d^{3}+\sqrt{md^{2}})\bigr)}
-Dikin-amenable.
Therefore, Theorem
2
implies that
\mathsf{GCDW}

incurs
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{2}(d^{3}+\sqrt{md^{2}}))=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}(d^{2}+\sqrt{m}))

total iterations of the
\mathsf{Dikin\ walk}
with
g
.

Now we bound the per-step complexity of the
\mathsf{Dikin\ walk}
(Algorithm
1
).
Recall that it requires (1) the update of the leverage scores, (2)
computation of the matrix function induced by the local metric
g
,
(3) the inverse of the matrix function and (4) its determinant. By

Lee and Sidford 2019
(with
p=2
and
d\leftarrow d_{s}

therein), the initialization of the leverage scores at the beginning
takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2\omega})
and their updates takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2(\omega-1)})

time. Since (1) takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2(\omega-1)})
, (2) takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{4}+md^{2(\omega-1)})
,
and (3) and (4) take
\mathcal{O}\left(d^{2\omega}\right)
, each iteration runs
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{2\omega}+md^{2(\omega-1)})
time. Even though the
initialization of leverage scores takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2\omega})
time,
the amortized per-step time complexity becomes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{2\omega}+md^{2(\omega-1)})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2(\omega-1)})

time, as the mixing rate is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}(d^{2}+\sqrt{m}))
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.3
.

We define
g_{X}=g=2(d^{2}g_{1}+g_{2})
,
where for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

g_{1}(X)=M^{\mathsf{T}}(X\otimes X)^{-1}M\qquad\text{and}\qquad g_{2}(X)=dc_{1}(\log m)^{c_{2}}M^{\mathsf{T}}A_{X}^{\mathsf{T}}W_{X}A_{X}M\,.

Since
d^{2}g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are SSC,
g
is also SSC due to
Lemma
4.5
and
\mathcal{O}^{*}(d^{3})
-symmetric due to
Lemma
4.11
. As
d^{2}g_{1}
and
g_{2}

is SLTSC and SASC,
g
is LTSC and ASC. Putting these together, it
follows that
g
is
{\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{*}(d^{3}),\mathcal{O}^{*}(d^{3})\bigr)}
-Dikin-amenable.
Therefore, Theorem
2
implies that
\mathsf{GCDW}

requires
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{5})
iterations of the
\mathsf{Dikin\ walk}
with
g
. Since
the initialization and update of the Lewis weight takes
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2\omega})

and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2(\omega-1)})
time
Lee and Sidford 2019
,
the same implementation with Theorem
6.2
also has
the time complexity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(md^{2(\omega-1)})
.
∎

7.5.2
Efficient implementation

Proof of Proposition
6.4
.

Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}

be a given vector, and denote
\bar{g}_{0}:=g_{1}
and
\bar{g}_{i}:=\bar{g}_{i-1}+u_{i}u_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}

for
i\in[m]
. We first prepare the column vectors
u_{i}
’s of

U=M^{\mathsf{T}}A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{X}^{-1}
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time and then initialize

\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}v
and
\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}u_{i}
for
i\in[m]
in

\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega})
time. For
u_{i}
’s, note that
S_{X}
can
be prepared in
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time, and thus
A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{X}^{-1}

takes
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time due to
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times m}
. Since
each row of
M^{\mathsf{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}\times d^{2}}
has at most two non-zero
entries, we can obtain
u_{i}
’s in
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time.

For
\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}v
and
\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}u_{i}
, we recall from
Lemma
5.20
that for a vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}

\displaystyle g_{1}^{-1}z

\displaystyle=M^{\dagger}(X\otimes X)(M^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}z=LN(X\otimes X)NL^{\mathsf{T}}z\,.

Since each row of
L^{\mathsf{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d_{s}}
has at most two
non-zero entries,
w:=L^{\mathsf{T}}z\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{2})

time. From the definition of
N
, it follows that
Nw=\textup{{vec}}{\bigl(\frac{1}{2}(W+W^{\mathsf{T}})\bigr)}

for
W:=\textup{{vec}}^{-1}(w)\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, which also can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{2})

time. For
\overline{W}:=\frac{1}{2}(W+W^{\mathsf{T}})
, it follows that

(X\otimes X)Nw=(X\otimes X)\textup{{vec}}(\overline{W})\underset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:Kronecker}-1}}{=}\textup{{vec}}(X\overline{W}X)\,,

which can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega})
time by the fast matrix
multiplication, and in a similar way we can compute
LN\,\textup{{vec}}(X\overline{W}X)

in
\mathcal{O}(d^{2})
time. Putting all these together,
\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}v

can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega})
time, and repeating this for

u_{j}
’s yields
\{\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}v,\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}u_{1},\dots,\bar{g}_{0}^{-1}u_{m}\}

in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega})
time.

Starting with these initializations, we recursively use the Sherman–Morrison
formula: for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}
,

\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}z=\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}z-\frac{\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}u_{i}u_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}z}{1+u_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}u_{i}}\,.

(7.29)

Using
\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}u_{j}
and
\bar{g}_{i-1}^{-1}v
from a
previous iteration, we can compute each of
\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}u_{j}

and
\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}v
in the current iteration in
\mathcal{O}(d^{2})

time, and thus each round for update takes
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time in
total. Since we iterate for
m
rounds, Algorithm
4

outputs
\bar{g}_{m}^{-1}v=g(X)^{-1}v
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})

time.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

Here we provide details
of Algorithm
5
in two stages – (1) sampling
from
\mathcal{N}{\bigl(0,\frac{r^{2}}{d}g(x)^{-1}\bigr)}
and (2) computation
of acceptance probability.

(1) Gaussian sampling:

For simplicity, we ignore
r^{2}/d
and illustrate how to draw
v\sim\mathcal{N}(0,g(X)^{-1})

without full computation of
g(X)^{-1}
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})

time.

Our approach is to compute
v:=g(X)^{-1}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]w
for
w\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d^{2}+m})
, which follows the Gaussian distribution
with covariance

\displaystyle g(X)^{-1}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]{\Bigl(g(X)^{-1}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]\Bigr)}^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle=g(X)^{-1}(BB^{\mathsf{T}}+CC^{\mathsf{T}})g(X)^{-1}g(X)^{-1}\,,

since
v
is a linear transformation of the Gaussian random variable

w
, and
BB^{\mathsf{T}}+CC^{\mathsf{T}}=g(X)
.

Denoting
w=(w_{b},w_{u})
for
w_{b}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d^{2}})
and

w_{u}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{m})
, we can show that
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]w
can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega}+md^{2})
time as follows:

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]w

\displaystyle=Bw_{b}+Uw_{c}=M^{\mathsf{T}}\underbrace{(X\otimes X)^{-1/2}w_{b}}_{\text{Use Lemma \ref{eq:sherman-morrison}}}+M^{\mathsf{T}}A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{X}^{-1}w_{c}

\displaystyle=M^{\mathsf{T}}{\Bigl(\textup{{vec}}{\bigl(X^{-1/2}\textup{{vec}}^{-1}(w_{b})\,X^{-1/2}\bigr)}+A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{X}^{-1}w_{c}\Bigr)}\,,

where
\textup{{vec}}{\bigl(X^{-1/2}\,\textup{{vec}}^{-1}(w_{b})\,X^{-1/2}\bigr)}
and
A^{\mathsf{T}}S_{X}^{-1}w_{u}

can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega})
and
\mathcal{O}(md^{2})
time,
respectively. Since each row of
M^{\mathsf{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}\times d^{2}}

has at most two non-zero entries,
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]w
can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega}+md^{2})
time. Using Algorithm
4
,
we obtain
v=g(X)^{-1}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B&U\end{array}\right]w
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})
time.

(2) Computation of acceptance probability.

We show that this step also takes
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})

time. To compute
\det g(X)
, we use Algorithm
4

to prepare
\{\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}u_{1},\dots,\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}u_{m}\}_{i=0}^{m}

at
X
and
Y=\textup{{svec}}^{-1}(y)
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})

time. Recall the matrix determinant lemma:

\det(A+uu^{\mathsf{T}})=(1+u^{\mathsf{T}}A^{-1}u)\,\det A\,.

Using the following recursive formula

\displaystyle\det(\bar{g}_{i+1})

\displaystyle=\det(\bar{g}_{i}+u_{i+1}u_{i+1}^{\mathsf{T}})=(1+u_{i+1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{g}_{i}^{-1}u_{i+1})\,\det\bar{g}_{i}\,,

we start with
\det\bar{g}_{0}=\det g_{1}=2^{d(d-1)/2}(\det X)^{-(d+1)}

(see Lemma
A.1
-7), which can be computed in
\mathcal{O}(d^{\omega})

time, and compute
\det g(X)
(and
\det g(Y)
in the same way)
in
\mathcal{O}(md^{\omega}+m^{2}d^{2})
time.
∎

7.5.3
Handling approximate Lewis weights

Proof of Lemma
6.6
.

We just reproduce
the proof of Lemma
2.3
. For
\pi\propto\exp(-f)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{K}
,
we denote

p_{x}=\mathcal{N}{\Bigl(x,\frac{r^{2}}{d}g(x)^{-1}\Bigr)},\qquad R_{x}(z)=\frac{p_{z}(x)}{p_{x}(z)}\frac{\pi(z)}{\pi(x)},\qquad A_{x}(z)=\min{\bigl(1,R_{x}(z)\,\mathbf{1}_{K}(z)\bigr)}\,.

Then the transition kernel of the
\mathsf{Dikin\ walk}
started at
x
can be written
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}(x,dz)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(1-\mathbb{E}_{p_{x}}[A_{x}(\cdot)])}_{=:r_{x}}\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}z)+A_{x}(z)\,p_{x}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\,.

Thus, for
x,y\in\textup{{int}}(K)

\displaystyle d_{\textrm{{TV}}}(P_{x},P_{y})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{r_{x}+r_{y}}{2}}_{\textsf{I}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\int|A_{x}(z)\,p_{x}(z)-A_{y}(z)\,p_{y}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z}_{\textsf{II}}\,.

∎

We note that
(1-\delta)\,\widetilde{g}_{2}\preceq g_{2}\preceq(1+\delta)\,\widetilde{g}_{2}

and thus

(1-\delta)\,\widetilde{g}\preceq g\preceq(1+\delta)\,\widetilde{g}\,,

(7.30)

and this implies
(1-\delta)\,I\preceq\widetilde{g}^{-1/2}g\widetilde{g}^{-1/2}\preceq(1+\delta)\,I
.
Hence,
(1-\delta)^{d^{2}/2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\det g}{\det\widetilde{g}}}\leq(1+\delta)^{d^{2}/2}

and

\displaystyle(1-\delta)^{d^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\det\widetilde{g}(z)}{\det\widetilde{g}(x)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\det g(z)}{\det g(x)}}\leq(1+\delta)^{d^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\det\widetilde{g}(z)}{\det\widetilde{g}(x)}}\,.

(7.31)

With this in mind, recall that

r_{x}=1-\mathbb{E}_{p_{x}}[A_{x}(\cdot)]=1-\int\min{\Bigl(1,\,\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{K}(z)\frac{\exp(-f(z))}{\exp(-f(x))}}_{\eqqcolon\textsf{A}}\underbrace{\frac{p_{z}(x)}{p_{x}(z)}}_{\eqqcolon\textsf{B}}\Bigr)}\,p_{x}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

We can bound
A
in a similar way by using (
7.30
).
As for
B
,

\log\text{{B}}=-\frac{d}{2r^{2}}({\|z-x\|}_{z}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{x}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}(\log\det\widetilde{g}(z)-\log\det\widetilde{g}(x))\,.

As in Lemma
2.3
, the second term can be bounded lower
by
\exp\left(-3\varepsilon\right)
using (
7.31
). The
first term can be lower-bounded by invoking ASC of
g
. To see this,
ignoring the normalization constant of
g_{x}

\displaystyle(*)=

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{1}{\Bigl({\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{r^{2}}{d}\Bigr)}\sqrt{\left\lvert\widetilde{g}(x)\right\rvert}\exp{\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}\bigr)}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{1}{\Bigl({\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{r^{2}}{d}\Bigr)}\sqrt{\left\lvert g(x)\right\rvert}\exp{\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sqrt{\left\lvert\frac{\widetilde{g}(x)}{g(x)}\right\rvert}\exp{\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}({\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2})\bigr)}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{1}{\Bigl({\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{r^{2}}{d}\Bigr)}\sqrt{\left\lvert g(x)\right\rvert}\exp{\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot(1+\delta)^{d^{2}/2}\exp{\bigl(\frac{\delta}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\bigr)}\,\mathrm{d}z\,.

Due to
{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\lesssim r^{2}
w.h.p., taking
\delta=\varepsilon/d^{10}

leads to

(*)\leq 2\int\mathbf{1}{\Bigl({\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{r^{2}}{d}\Bigr)}\sqrt{\left\lvert g(x)\right\rvert}\exp{\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\bigr)}\,\mathrm{d}z.

Also, due to

\displaystyle{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{\widetilde{g}(x)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta)\,{\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}-(1+\delta)\,{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)\,({\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2})-2\delta\,{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\,,

we have

\displaystyle(*)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int\mathbf{1}{\Bigl({\|z-x\|}_{g(z)}^{2}-{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}\leq(2\varepsilon(1-\delta)^{-1}+\varepsilon)\,\frac{r^{2}}{d}\Bigr)}\sqrt{\left\lvert g(x)\right\rvert}e^{-\frac{1}{2}{\|z-x\|}_{g(x)}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq 6\varepsilon

by invoking ASC of
g
in the last inequality. Putting these together,

\mathsf{I}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
. For
\mathsf{II}
, we can follow the
proof of Lemma
2.3
to show
\mathsf{II}\leq\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
,
and every technical issue can be resolved by repeating the same techniques
above.
